### Rules that apply to every bench session

1. Identify the chassis and physical chip before attaching anything.
2. For resistance/continuity measurements, disconnect all external power inputs and cables. Record/remove the coin-cell where needed for an unpowered board; photograph its orientation first. Confirm no residual DC voltage on the nodes before changing to resistance mode.
3. Wait for stored charge to discharge. Do not short rails or capacitors to discharge them.
4. Use the V/ohm meter input, not a current input. Record actual resistance values rather than only a beep.
5. Do not bridge unknown headers, ground unknown control pins, or assume a header's function from its label.
6. The identified G2 flashes require **1.8 V power and compatible logic signals**. A supply regulator alone is not proof of signal-level translation.
7. Keep router power, console, and USB disconnected during external-programmer work. Do not address bus contention by powering the router and programmer together or increasing voltage.
8. Do not use the SOIC-8 clip on half of a SOIC-16 package. Do not use oversized meter hooks on fine IC legs.
9. For any later powered board measurement, restore mounting and cooling, secure insulated measurement leads while power is off, and select DC volts before power. Keep measurements on the low-voltage logic board; do not probe inside a mains PSU.
10. Preserve original dumps, board identity data, configurations, and credentials privately. A full main-array flash backup does not necessarily include OTP or separate security/configuration registers.

| 3 / C8375-01 | Map C8375 P1_CSPI/P2_CSPI unpowered | First task with fine probes | Separate connection tables for each flash | Trace mux/series paths or wait for SOIC-16 clip |
| 4 / P-01 | Validate programmer and adapter separately | Before any target attachment | Circuit identification, orientation, voltage record | Correct the setup before connecting a router |
| 5 / R-01 | Obtain two stable full reads per physical chip | After electrical checks | Size, hash, byte comparison, credible data | Diagnose wiring, loading, mux access or isolation; no write |
| 6 / C8375-02 | Identify the C8375 active target | After stable reads | Comparison against original and known nonbooting references | Inspect differences; do not force a bank assignment |
| 7 / C8375-03 | Restore exact C8375 original image | After target identification and preserved backups | Full readback equals original, then logged boot test | Separate programming mismatch from persistent boot failure |
| 8 / C8235-01 | Baseline and back up each C8235 independently | In parallel with preparation only where practical | Per-chassis observations and both-chip backups | Continue acquisition and mapping before firmware experiments |
| 9 / C8300-02 | Inspect C8300 logic board and identify storage | If console checks remain silent | Photos, memory/connector inspection, identified flash parts | Choose the next hardware test from observed evidence |

Each task remains **planned** until a dated result is entered. A failed test is useful evidence; record it before changing the setup.

Values near the meter lead baseline support a direct connection. Higher resistance may indicate a series resistor or another circuit path. A kΩ reading does not establish a direct trace. An unpowered mux may leave a valid route disconnected; an open reading alone does not prove a failed trace or component.

### Micron package reference

The previously identified part is **MT25QU256ABA8ESF-0SIT / RW170**, 256 Mbit / 32 MiB, 1.7–2.0 V operating supply, SOIC-16 wide body. Confirm the marking on the unit being measured.

The following is the **IC pin reference, top view, pin-configuration option 8** from the [Micron datasheet, Figure 6](https://www.mouser.com/datasheet/2/671/mt25q_qljs_u_256_aba_0-3178731.pdf). It is **not** a verified header or programmer-adapter pinout.

| Flash pin | Signal | Role in ordinary single-data-line SPI |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | DQ3/HOLD# | Hold control; establish suitable inactive level |
| 2 | VCC | 1.8 V supply |
| 3 | RESET# | Reset control; establish suitable inactive level |
| 7 | S# | Chip select |
| 8 | DQ1 | MISO, data from flash |
| 9 | W#/DQ2 | Write-protect/control input |
| 10 | VSS | Ground |
| 15 | DQ0 | MOSI, data to flash |
| 16 | C | SPI clock |

Pins **4, 5, 6, 11, 12, 13, 14** are DNU: do not connect them. Check board pull-ups and other drivers before deciding how HOLD#, RESET#, and W# will be handled. Do not hard-short an unknown board-driven control.

### Mux investigation — next unpowered checks

In the saved inspection crop, U3_CSPI and U4_CSPI are the smaller parts beside P2_CSPI/P1_CSPI, visibly marked PI3CH480QE. The [manufacturer datasheet](https://www.mouser.com/datasheet/2/322/PI3CH480-348795.pdf) identifies a four-channel 2:1 mux/demux supporting 1.8 V operation: pin 1 Select, pin 8 GND, pin 15 active-low Enable, pin 16 VCC. Disabled outputs are high impedance. This establishes component behavior, not actual board routing or the cause of the measured isolation.

Next identify **U4_CSPI**, the lower small mux beside P1 in the crop, and its pin-1 marker before probing. In that photo the marker appears at the upper-right package corner; for this orientation the top row runs right-to-left 1–8 and the bottom row left-to-right 9–16. Confirm the physical marker; do not reuse the flash-package orientation. First measure **U4 pin 8 (top-left corner lead in that orientation) to verified P1 header pin 2** in resistance mode, with all cords disconnected, CR2032 removed, and programmer unattached. A ground result provides an orientation/contact check before tracing mux data or supply pins. Actual mux-ground result, supply connection, select/enable wiring, and header-to-mux routes remain pending. Do not power or bridge unknown control pins.

**U4 ground result received:** the user reports **0.6 Ω from U4_CSPI pin 8 to P1_CSPI header pin 2**, matching the fine-tip baseline. This supports the mux-ground connection and is consistent with the identified orientation. The user explicitly reports that this chip is very small and maintaining probe contact is difficult. Limit the next task to one corner contact: **U4_CSPI pin 16 (bottom-right in the saved crop) to P1_CSPI header pin 1**. The purpose is to determine whether the mux uses the same supply net as the flash; no such connection is established yet. Keep all sources disconnected. If single-leg contact cannot be made reliably, stop fine-pin probing and move to identifying/validating the unattached programmer and adapter rather than forcing contact. No hooks on closely spaced mux legs and no control-pin jumpers are requested.

**U4 supply-net result received:** after multiple attempts the user reports **1.22 kΩ from U4_CSPI pin 16 (mux VCC) to P1_CSPI header pin 1 (mapped flash VCC)**. This is not a demonstrated direct shared supply connection; do not wire these nets together or assume applying 1.8 V at P1 pin 1 will operate the mux. The unpowered measurement does not establish U4's operating voltage, prove a fault, or prove mux isolation as the cause of the signal readings. Combined with difficult access and no direct clock/CS# paths found, pause fine mux probing and move to **P-01: actual programmer/adapter identification and standalone validation**. Request clear photographs of both sides of the received CH341A board and 1.8 V adapter with USB unplugged and no router, flash clip, or target attached. Exact board revision, adapter direction, level-translation circuitry, jumper settings, and output points must be established before powered bench measurements. Header signal mapping remains incomplete; do not attach the programmer to P1 based only on its known flash supply/ground connections.

**Expanded chip-select scan result:** the user went from **U1 flash pin 7 to every U4 pin and every P1/P2 post** and found no readings below about **5 kΩ**. Individual endpoint readings were not supplied; do not turn the approximate minimum into exact per-pin values. The three originally requested U4 common-terminal pairs are covered by this report and should not be repeated. This leaves CS# routing unresolved, without establishing a component fault. Micron Figure 6 confirms the package's pin 7 is S#.

**User proposal: hybrid clip/header access and CH341A diagnostic probing:** the user cannot find the CS# connection after trying many points (no new exact values or complete U3-specific scope supplied). They ask whether the SOIC-8 clip could cover some SOIC-16 pins while Dupont/header connections supply the rest, and whether guessed powered wiring can permanently damage hardware.

**Assessment:** a half-package clip fit is possible in principle only if this particular clip fits the wide body and aligns/grips each intended leg securely. At the CS# end, away from the pin-1 dot, the candidate four-per-side window is flash pins 5–8 and 9–12. It includes CS# 7, MISO 8, WP# 9 and GND 10; touched DNU pins 5/6/11/12 must remain electrically disconnected. The ordinary eight-pin clip cable cannot be used straight through: individual contact mapping and a suitable breakout are required. P1-1 flash supply and P1-2 ground are mapped, and P1-4 reaches the clock mux IA0 branch, but mux power/select/enable and MOSI are still unresolved. The proposal does not establish a complete ready-to-power harness.

**Electrical risk answer:** yes, guessed wiring can permanently damage parts even while normal router power is unplugged; the CH341A supplies/drives the connected circuit from USB. An ID-only command does not prevent voltage, short-circuit, output-contention or back-powering damage. Keep USB and normal router power disconnected for each wiring change; use the accepted 1.8 V adapter output. Identification and stable reads become useful diagnostics after a known compatible wiring/loading setup exists.

**Clip photographs received:** four originals, IMG_1269/1270/1271/1273, are indexed in [Photos/README](Photos/README.md). They show the correct U1/CS-end placement appears mechanically possible and reveal the separate green clip breakout's eight male legs for individual wiring. This breakout is distinct from the 1.8 V adapter. Partly hidden jaw contacts prevent an electrical fit/shorts conclusion from the photos alone. No new per-pin readings were supplied.

**October 25 clip report and clarified endpoints:** the user says the changed clip is lined up clip 1→U1-5, clip 4→U1-8, clip 5→U1-9, clip 8→U1-12. They report all other clip contacts OL and clip 5 at 0.3 Ω to ground, then explicitly confirm the fixed probe was **P1 pin 2/GND**. Record the alignment separately from these ground-scan results; no direct clip-to-corresponding-chip-leg continuity values have been supplied. A non-ground contact reading OL to ground is not automatically a bad connection. Clip 5's low reading does not prove it physically lands on U1 pin 9; pin 9 WP# could be held low by the board, the assumed map/contact may differ, or a bridge may be involved. U1 pin 10 remains the package's ground reference.

**Ground numbering correction received:** the user explicitly says the previous assignment was mistaken: **U1 pin 10, via clip contact 6**, is ground, confirmed on the bare chip and again after reseating the clip; the other checked contacts are OL to ground. Attribute the prior 0.3 Ω ground reading to the corrected contact. This supersedes the pin-9/contact-5 report and the native-WP#-ground investigation; no WP# tie/clip bridge/component fault is established from the withdrawn numbering. Do not request that old ground test again. Direct non-ground contact measurements are still needed before powered wiring.

**Blocked pad access and pairwise result:** the user confirms the clip blocks the actual chip legs/pads, so the two direct non-ground contact checks are unperformed. They explicitly withdraw the broad claim that every clip-contact pair is OL. The specific requested **clip contact 3→clip contact 4 result is OL**. Preserve only that confirmed pair; it rules out a measured low-resistance connection there but is not proof of correct individual contact.

**CS# reference check received:** user measures **clip contact 3 to P1 pin 8 = 9.33 kΩ**, matching C8375-01-CS-SCAN's bare U1 pin 7 to P1 pin 8 value. This supports intended CS# contact through the fitted clip; it is not unique proof, a powered test, or an assignment of P1-8 as CS#. Keep the reference result and do not repeat it.

**Data trace result and access limit:** user reports the requested U1-MOSI-15→U4-7/9/12 and expected clip-MISO-4→U4-7/9/12 pairs all MΩ or OL, but says U4 is too tiny to contact reliably. Individual results/values were not supplied. Do not infer failed/disconnected routes from this attempted group scan. Stop repeating U4 fine-pin checks with the current physical access.

**Delivery clarification:** the SOIC-16 clip has not arrived; user says approximately 16 more hours, around **4:30 PM October 25, America/New_York**. This is a user-provided expectation, not a verified delivery event.

**Next requested action — assess direct access with existing hooks:** a properly fitting full SOIC-16 clip would bypass the header/mux. Before it arrives, two suitably small secure hooks could potentially reach the uncovered larger U1 flash **pin 15/MOSI and pin 16/CLK** directly while the partial clip supplies CS#/MISO. Fit is unverified, the adjacent leads must remain electrically isolated, and remaining controls/loading are unresolved. Request a photo of the actual hook tips beside U1's two lower-left leads, with all normal router power and programmer USB disconnected and hooks left unattached. Do not force them on, substitute hand-held write contacts, energize the proposed wiring, or infer suitability from the 4 mm connector size. See the [direct-access proposal](C8375-CH341A-HEADER-WIRING.md). No further tiny-mux sweep is requested.

The sheet makes the known versus missing harness assignments explicit. After a working route and power/control setup are established, proceed to R-01: two matching plausible full reads per physical chip, comparison against the preserved exact original, then physical-target selection before restoration.

**Actual received hardware, photo inspection:** the black board is labeled CH341A Mini Programmer, with revision text appearing as `V1.512`, an external crystal, a 3.3 V-marked 1117 regulator, a 16-position ZIF socket, and underside legends for `BIOS 25 SPI`, `EEPROM 24 I2C`, CLK/CS/MOSI/MISO/GND/3.3 V/5 V. Its printed jumper legend specifies **1–2 programming, 2–3 TTL**; confirm those actual numbered posts while USB is unplugged rather than inferring a shunt position from perspective.

The green adapter has a blue ZIF socket, underside male contacts, an **1117-marked regulator** whose output variant is not established from its visible code, and a TI-marked 48-pin IC whose marking appears consistent with **74LVC16T245**. The [TI family reference](https://www.ti.com/product/SN74LVC16T245) describes dual-supply translation with configurable direction and 1.65–5.5 V rails. This is a candidate identification, not authentication of the installed IC or verification of the module's wiring, regulator output, input/output orientation, pin mapping, or operating levels. Target supply and signal checks remain pending.

It warned that driver detachment lacked entitlements/root privilege, then configured interface 1 and reached no-chip-found. A local follow-up, `flashrom -VV -p ch341a_spi -c MT25QU256`, executed without sudo, reported **device revision 3.0.4**, initialized SPI, and received an all-FF ID without USB transfer errors. Exit 1 is expected for this no-target probe; it is not a valid chip detection or read. The detach warning was nonfatal in these observed runs and did not require a permissions/security change. Preserve future errors individually rather than assuming warnings always prevent or never affect access. [Complete command evidence](recovery-prep-20261024/CH341A-BARE-USB-VERIFICATION.txt).

It again reported no chip, expected with the programmer empty. No additional driver or macOS security changes are indicated by these results. Adapter and target-side electrical validation remain pending.

Some CH341A boards drive signals near 5 V despite a lower socket supply; see [official flashrom documentation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/flashrom/flashrom/main/doc/supported_hw/supported_prog/ch341ab.rst).

Example commands, **deferred until electrical setup is verified**. Use the exact chip name supported and identified by the installed build; `MT25QU256` is the expected family name, not a substitute for identification. Run in a new directory per chassis/chip and do not overwrite earlier reads:

```sh
flashrom -p ch341a_spi
flashrom -p ch341a_spi -c MT25QU256 -r read1.bin
flashrom -p ch341a_spi -c MT25QU256 -r read2.bin
wc -c read1.bin read2.bin
shasum -a 256 read1.bin read2.bin
cmp read1.bin read2.bin
```

`cmp` returning success with no output means byte-for-byte equality. Failed identification, inconsistent reads, or suspicious contents lead back to connection/isolation diagnosis. No erase, write, unlock, OTP, or configuration-modification command is included in this acquisition procedure.

The original backup was captured with manual ROMMON register `0x1820`; stopping at ROMMON after restoration can be expected and is not, by itself, a failed restore. If original ROMMON returns, inspect the environment and BOOT target before re-establishing the previously tested settings:

```text
USER_BOOT_PARAM=maxcpus=16 failure_shell initcall_blacklist=octeontx_edac_init
BOOT=bootflash:packages.conf,12;
Configuration register=0x1822
```

Use syntax and full history from [the consolidated C8375 context](C8375-G2-RECOVERY-CONTEXT.md). The EDAC blacklist avoided a reporting-driver crash; it did not establish fault-free memory. Do not blindly substitute `0x2102` or repeat the incompatible release-ROMMON experiment.

**Interpretation:** a programming/readback mismatch means restoration is not verified. If readback matches but boot still fails, preserve that distinction and investigate power/reset, bank routing, boot state, and remaining hardware faults. Returning to IOS is an initial recovery milestone; fan/PSU behavior, stability, and functional testing remain separate work.

## 7. Three C8235s: preserve and compare before choosing firmware

### C8235-01 — Record an independent baseline for each chassis

**October 25 correction, America/New_York:** user confirms the current console-connected unit is the previously worked C8235 (`FGL2835L42B`) but says earlier C8235 measurements were taken on a different unit described as an exact replica. That measured chassis is not yet bound to C8235-B or C8235-C. Preserve earlier readings, but do not apply their physical-chip/header attribution to C8235-A without independent identification. Historical disassembly does not establish today's console unit's assembly/battery state. User answered yes to the secured board/cooling and no attached programmer/probes/jumpers check; coin-cell and other cable/USB-storage state were not separately supplied.

At **12:02 EDT**, after checking that no process owned the port, opened `/dev/cu.usbserial-AL00F7EQ` at **115200 8N1, no flow control** with `c8235_serial_console.py`. Local original capture: `router-console-logs/20261025-120203-console.log`. A one-shot uppercase `B` response is armed for `Press 'B' within 10 seconds for boot menu`. **Observed:** power-on banner identifies mirabile-arata r2 / A0 alt package, boot stub 2026-04-01, and `SPI0_CS0,REMOTE, using SPI0_CS0`. Automatic `B` reached Marvell Boot Options. Sent `N` (Boot Normally) at about **12:03 EDT**; memory initialization and BL2/BL31 completed, followed by `Fatal error found on TPM secure device while accessing manufacturing status, cannot boot the system!`. Cisco ROMMON and IOS XE were not reached. This reproduces the primary-path blocker; it does not establish the TPM fault's cause or the golden bank's condition. [Sanitized capture](sanitized-evidence/router-console-logs/20261025-120203-console.log). No firmware/configuration/security writes were performed. Last observed state: powered at the halt; the capture was subsequently closed before changing chassis, and user subsequently confirmed powering A off before changing cables. Next: preserve separate full-chip reads before choosing a restore candidate; earlier USB boot is unavailable through this primary path.

**Historical preparation checkpoint (superseded by C8235-B results below):** user reiterates that of the two other C8235s, one is taken apart and one remains fully assembled. Serial-to-C8235-B/C assignments remain pending. User proposes connecting the assembled unit with USB storage and serial to test 26.2.1; instructed to keep it powered off while changing cables and report when connected. Fresh capture and inspection of its actual ROMMON precede any manual USB boot. No startup or boot outcome for this unit has been observed.

**C8235-B baseline, October 25, from 12:08 EDT:** user confirmed the cable/USB move with the other unit assembled and off. Fresh capture at `/dev/cu.usbserial-AL00F7EQ`, **115200 8N1/no flow**, produced clear output and reached the Marvell menu via automatic `B`. Normal boot (`N`) reached development ROMMON `SDK112312-SoC1-SB-v12.1.22`, Boot ROM0, then autobooted `bootflash:packages.conf` to IOS XE **17.18.3a**. Console identifies **C8230X-8M2X / FGL2835LBHU**, Arata r2 / A0 alt package, 16 GB; bind this assembled chassis to **C8235-B**. Remaining disassembled chassis is **C8235-C**, serial pending. Reported bootflash free space is **0 kB**; startup logs include TPM self-test error 256, mailbox errors and firmware-reported `12V_DG_ST` 0 mV/Critical_Low. No physical voltage was measured. `cpp_cp_svr` failed with **rc139**, F0 offline, despite CLI access. `show version` reports confreg **0x3922**.

USB `c8kg2be-universalk9.26.02.01.SPA.bin` is **1,048,008,916 bytes**. IOS `verify /sha512` matched `shasum -a 512` of the local original; exact digest is in the capture and [context](C8235-G2-RECOVERY-CONTEXT.txt). Issued `reload`/confirmation; automatic three Ctrl-C bytes on `UUID Len: 0xC` reached `rommon 1 >`. `showmon -v` confirmed development ROMMON. ROMMON `dir usb0:/<filename>` rejected a file as a directory; `dir usb0:` succeeded. Issued **`boot usb0:/c8kg2be-universalk9.26.02.01.SPA.bin`**, and image reading began; outcome pending at this checkpoint. No flash/config-save/confreg change or deletion issued. Evidence: local original `router-console-logs/20261025-120812-console.log`; public counterpart generated beneath `sanitized-evidence/`.

**C8235-B 26.2.1 result, about 12:16 EDT:** USB boot reached `Router>` and `Router#`; `show version` confirms **26.02.1**, USB image, `FGL2835LBHU`, and unchanged **0x3922**. Development ROMMON remains in place. This verifies boot/CLI access only. 26.2.1 reports full `/bootflash/sysboot` and packages using RAM; directory reports **20,229,492,736 bytes total / 0 free**. `core/` has **81 listed regular files, 8,484,220,577 bytes**, including **7,954,014,208 bytes of uncompressed report .tar files**; these are file sizes, not allocated-disk usage. No deletion/copy-off performed. Brief RP status shows **26% RAM used / 74% free**. Sensor output CPU **52 C**, inlet **24 C**, outlet **22 C**, other listed rails Normal, with `12V_DG_ST` still firmware-reported **0 mV / Critical_Low**; no meter verification or hardware-fault conclusion.

About two minutes into startup, `show platform` still reports **F0 init, active / 0/0 booting**, with **20/40-second punt-keepalive failures**, mailbox/TPM self-test errors and full-storage write errors. Storage exhaustion may contribute, but cause is unproven; no functional/stability pass. Router dates February 2024 / May 2025 are incorrect; actual work date is October 25, 2026 EDT. Capture remains active, with three Ctrl-C bytes armed on `UUID Len: 0xC` for any subsequent reboot. [Sanitized session capture](sanitized-evidence/router-console-logs/20261025-120812-console.log). Next: preserve crash evidence, plan a scoped bootflash cleanup, and retest; preserve development ROMMON and obtain full-chip backups separately.

**Later 26.2.1 failure, captured in the 12:18 EDT poll:** `cpp_cp_svr` was held down and failed critically with **rc139**, followed by **F0 offline**. Router-clock event `18:51:48` is about two minutes after CLI access; it is not the actual session time. A whole-chassis reload was not yet observed at this checkpoint. The USB boot succeeded, but packet-processing stability failed. Matching rc139 under both software versions does not identify the underlying cause.

**12:21 EDT publication checkpoint:** received 26.2.1 output continues through **280 seconds** of missed punt keepalives and full-storage warnings; no whole-chassis reload has appeared. Firmware also reports `flash:core value 84% (16674 MB)`; this is a separately reported warning, not the earlier sum of directory-listed bytes. Public captures are sanitized snapshots at sync time; original logs remain local/ignored while capture continues. Owner requests ongoing tracking and GitHub sync at meaningful checkpoints and handoffs.


**Full reload observed, 12:23 EDT console poll:** after **300 seconds** without punt keepalives, 26.2.1 reported software-forced reload, `Aborted(6)` in **PuntInject Keepalive Process**, critical `linux_iosd-image` **rc134**, and system reload. Internal crashinfo writes failed with zero space; firmware reported writing **`usb0:crashinfo_20250529-185458-UTC`**. Presence/content/hash of that new file remain unverified; its filename follows the incorrect router clock. The unchanged July 2024 development ROMMON returned, and the armed three Ctrl-C bytes stopped autoboot at **`rommon 1 >`**. Current observed state: powered at Cisco ROMMON, capture active. This supersedes the earlier no-whole-chassis-reload checkpoints while retaining them as intermediate observations.

**Next proposed C8235-B test:** preserve and verify relevant crash reports on external storage, free sufficient bootflash from specifically selected preserved reports, then repeat the same verified 26.2.1 USB boot and observe F0 readiness/keepalives beyond the previous 300-second failure. The firmware recommends **5,926,609 kB** free; aim for at least **6 GB** before the repeat. No file transfer/deletion or cleanup test has yet occurred. Retain installed firmware/configuration and development ROMMON. This storage test can precede SOIC-16 clip delivery; the underlying cause remains unresolved.

**Measured resource mismatch, October 31, 16:15 EDT checkpoint:** B's unchanged development firmware exposes **32 AF loopback VFs** in the live tree and PCI attributes. Native 26.2.1 drivers allocate six PFs + 32 VFs successfully with `nix_maxlf=128`. Cisco's hash-matched ARATA startup script assumes **16 loopbacks**, unbinds/rebinds interfaces and lowers `nix_maxlf` to **32**. Reproduced exhaustion with five PFs + 32 VFs; five VF binds fail -28. A single-VF detach/rebind releases/reuses its slot, so a simple leak is not demonstrated. AF `sriov_numvfs` writes were rejected; count stays 32. Unbinding VF16–VF31 and retaining VF0–VF15 succeeds with **21 allocations at limit 32**. These bounded runtime mutations followed the measured mismatch under step 6; the initial inventory's no-mutation rule remains appropriate before that evidence. [Full record and limitations](c8235-recovery/crash-review-20261031/REVIEW.md#isolated-resource-experiment--october-31-afternoon-edt), [hashes/state](c8235-recovery/crash-review-20261031/resource-experiment.json).

**Ordered next tests — planned unless checked below:**

1. **Current B baseline.** Reconfirm FGL2835LBHU, normal assembly/cooling, battery, cables and exclusive console ownership. Preserve the installed image/manifest, startup/running configuration privately, boot variables and ROMMON version. First observe its current prompt; do not assume the October 25 ROMMON state is current. Use 115200/8N1/no flow once confirmed. If a new boot is needed, use the previously verified 26.2.1 USB image with unchanged ROMMON, after rechecking its hash and bootflash free space. Capture one complete boot/failure rather than leave it looping.
2. **Measure the resource failure.** Capture `show version`, `show inventory`, `show platform`, `show environment all`, `show platform software status control-processor brief` and `show platform software infra punt-keepalive` where supported. Collect the earliest kernel/CPP/QFP traces, using CLI help to verify retrieval syntax. With an established read-only shell method, inventory PCI PF/VF bindings, SR-IOV counts and exposed RVU/NIX capacity/free/assigned resource diagnostics, plus the selected board profile/device tree. Discover existing endpoints first; do not guess register addresses or write `sriov_numvfs`, unbind drivers or reset devices. Compare allocations before versus after CPP startup. Keep original diagnostics and core files private.
3. **Temporary configuration control on B.** After private backups, use the actual ROMMON's verified startup-config-ignore mechanism for one otherwise identical 26.2.1 boot; do not erase or save over the existing configuration. Record the exact original setting (historically confreg **0x3922**) and restoration procedure before changing it. Remove external traffic as a separate documented variable if present; do not combine config, image and peripheral changes in one trial. If the same early NIX failures remain, prioritize platform initialization over user features. Restore the original setting after the comparison.
4. **Independent C baseline.** Identify its actual serial/PID/revision and confirm safe assembly/cooling before power. Preserve its original ROMMON/environment and capture its untouched installed startup first. If appropriate, compare the exact same verified image with the same minimal test conditions. Compare NIX limits/allocations, board profile, silicon revision and firmware versions. A stable C is a control, not a whole-chip donor; a matching failure supports a shared issue without proving the cause.
5. **One justified IOS comparison.** Repeating 26.1.1 alone now offers limited new information because an archived report already shows the same failure. Select one genuinely different C8235-supported image after checking its release notes, exact file/hash, prototype caveats and whether boot/install changes firmware automatically. Earliest supported 17.18.1a or a later 17.18 maintenance release are candidates for investigation, not selected fixes. Neither is currently identified on the USB. Do not substitute C8375's 17.15.6 or classic `c8000be` firmware. Preserve IOx/application data and account for Cisco's documented 26.2-to-earlier disk-layout change before downgrading. Prefer a manual reversible image boot where supported; no ROMMON/CPLD/security update in this stage.
6. **Target the demonstrated difference.** If a resource/profile mismatch is established, identify a board-specific supported correction or reversible runtime experiment, with exact old/new values and rollback, before applying it. If hardware differences or errors emerge, perform properly isolated physical/memory/power tests separately. Firmware writes require stable full backups and exact target/changed/preserved-region evidence; keep C8235-A's TPM-halt recovery separate. Do not suppress keepalives/watchdogs as a stability fix.

**Acceptance and stop rules:** first require F0 and its subslot to become operational, no NIX allocation/port-attachment failure, no CPP restart and no missing punt keepalives for **15 minutes** on the host clock. Stop a trial on the first clear recurrence and preserve its report. A CLI prompt or surviving in ROMMON/maintenance Linux is not a pass. After the first pass, run **60 minutes idle**, then **60 minutes controlled two-port forwarding** with packet counters/loss/errors, temperatures, memory and bootflash tracked; use appropriate isolated test addressing. Finally complete **three cold boots** and an **overnight soak** before calling it stable. These are planned acceptance gates, not completed tests or a guarantee of reliability.

### C8235-02 — Improve mapping on the worked Arata board

The historical Arata photo/inspection record identifies flashes U2900/U2901 and nearby PI3CH480 muxes U2903/U2904. It previously attributed the associated measurements to C8235-A. On October 25 the user clarified that those measurements were on another replica; its serial remains unresolved, so do not transfer measured connectivity to A or B. J2900 remains unidentified. Historical measurements supported one ground pin but did not establish a direct connection from the other pin to mux Select; the reported approximately 6.5 kΩ reading was difficult to obtain with large probes.

- [ ] Repeat uncertain resistance measurements with fine probes and a recorded lead baseline.
- [ ] Map J2900/J2901 to known flash and mux pins where accessible.
- [ ] Verify exact mux pin orientation against the PI3CH480 datasheet before probing.
- [ ] Record direct paths, resistive paths, and open readings without equating them to a ROM selector.
- [ ] Consider powered DC measurements only after safe mounting/cooling and secure lead attachment are practical.

## 8. C8300: determine what “no console” means

### C8300-01 — External console baseline

**November 2 live result, completed 17:09 EST:** [session evidence](c8300-recovery/console-20261102/results.json). Owner confirmed assembled state and installed coin-cell; exclusive receive-only captures. RJ45 9600 and 115200 startups received zero bytes for 341.379 s and 312.987 s after owner reports; RJ45 capture closed at 16:42:29. Native USB enumerated as Cisco USB Console. The earlier 115200-to-9600 observation remains historical; the owner subsequently requested a fresh uninterrupted 115200 cold-start capture. Owner reported off/waiting 30 seconds, then on at the 17:04:15 report marker; **zero bytes for 306.529 s**, green/red/green main indicators and green right USB-console light. Native capture closed at 17:09:21 without error; lsof confirms port released. Physical RJ45 disconnection during native USB use was not explicitly restated. No ten-minute checkpoint or all-9600 native cold start claimed. Owner subsequently confirms power off, all cables disconnected, cover still on, chassis serial FDO25150HVC; new internal photo identifies the 32 GB DIMM. Inspection/reseat and cover restoration requested; completion and post-reseat capture pending.

Cisco lists steady red STATUS as booting and blinking red as a hardware-integrity error. Persistent steady red with silence warrants investigation but does not by itself prove an irreparable router. Green power/fan indications do not validate every CPU or memory rail.

### C8300-02 — Unpowered physical inspection alongside console diagnosis

**November 2, 18:34 EST PSU inventory follow-up:** owner reports C8300 off/unplugged and one **PWR-CC1-250WAC** installed. The two-installed-supply proposal below cannot be used on that reported setup. Cisco explicitly lists this PSU model for classic C8300-1N1S. A potential substitute is **LIT2852A9TJ**, historically booting C8375-A on October 31; current availability/location/state and distinction from the C8300 supply are unconfirmed. Ask those facts before moving hardware. If available, compare in the same slot using unchanged cord/outlet, DIMM and covered cooling, with a fresh captured startup. Donor G2 fan/recognition issues remain separate; historical boot does not fully qualify PSU health. No hardware action or console access occurred. Owner also says chassis only supports one PSU; retain that report without overriding Cisco’s dual-slot documentation or assuming a second installed supply. [Current and original conditional plans](c8300-recovery/psu-comparison-20261102/results.json).

**November 2, 18:29 EST alternate-test plan:** owner asks for a test while deciding whether to buy memory. Propose a **conditional PSU source comparison**: after verifying two suitable installed AC supplies and present state, keep both fitted/cover closed, use the same cord/outlet to power one identified PSU at a time, and compare fresh captured startups at unchanged settings. Switch off/remove AC between runs; no live removal or internal probing. A difference implicates a supply/slot path; unchanged outcomes do not validate all internal rails. PSU count/models/current feeds and on/off state requested; no new capture or test performed. If only one supply is present, select a confirmed compatible alternative or qualified board measurement instead. [Detailed planned controls and limits](c8300-recovery/psu-comparison-20261102/results.json).

**November 2, 18:18 EST proposed-memory comparison:** owner is considering a described **16 GB DDR4-2400 / PC4-19200 registered ECC RDIMM, 2Rx4, 1.2 V, 288 pins** and asks how it compares with the installed Micron. Listed type, rank organization, voltage and pin count match; proposed capacity is half (16 versus 32 GB), with about 10% lower rated data rate (2400 versus 2666 MT/s). Cisco documents a 16 GB option; that capacity is suitable for a correctly qualified comparison module. Actual operating speed, exact part identity and C8300 qualification remain unknown. Listing URL/manufacturer/part number requested; no order, insertion or new boot reported. [Recorded comparison](research-evidence/c8300-a-review-20261102/memory-review.json).

**November 2, 18:15 EST spare-inventory update:** owner reports many DDR3 modules and a possible non-ECC spare; DDR4 availability is uncertain. No qualified replacement or exact candidate part number is available yet. DDR3 is not interchangeable with the DDR4 slot; ordinary non-ECC desktop DDR4 does not match the installed registered ECC module and is not selected as a diagnostic substitute. Request the full part number of any DDR4 candidate before installation; ECC alone, capacity and physical fit do not establish qualification. Cisco lists 8/16/32 GB options, so an exact 32 GB capacity match is not the only possible supported comparison. No substitution, new boot, programmer connection or purchase occurred. [Screening criteria and sources](research-evidence/c8300-a-review-20261102/memory-review.json).

**November 2, 17:55 EST captured result:** capture was armed before the owner-reported restart. From the **17:50:27** power-on report through close at **17:55:39**, native USB **115200 / 8N1 / no flow** received **zero bytes for 311.953 s**, with no capture errors and no transmitted bytes/BREAK. Owner confirms fresh-cycle green/red/green and right USB console-active green. This matches the pre-reseat 115200 native USB result; reseating produced no observed improvement and does not rule out defective RAM. Capture closed and aliases released. A known-working compatible DIMM comparison is the next memory test if available; availability requested, no replacement selected. Last confirmed router state is on/assembled/native USB connected/RJ45 unplugged; power-off suggested, not confirmed. [Full post-reseat events](c8300-recovery/console-20261102/post-reseat-native-usb-events.jsonl), [comparison](c8300-recovery/console-20261102/README.md).

**November 2, 17:35–17:37 EST reseat update:** owner reports memory looks clean/new, no brown/black spots and pins look fine; **same DIMM reseated and clicked into place**. Visual condition does not establish electrical health. Initial pre-removal latch/alignment state was not supplied. Owner powered on before capture, again reports green/red/green, then explicitly confirms **cover secured, native USB connected, RJ45 unplugged**. Fresh independent enumeration/ownership checks pass; exclusive receive-only capture armed at **115200 / 8N1 / no flow** at **17:36:05**. One captured off-at-least-30-seconds/on comparison is requested; no early console result is claimed for the first post-reseat start. [Live test record](c8300-recovery/console-20261102/results.json), [inspection report](research-evidence/c8300-a-review-20261102/memory-review.json).

**November 2, 17:22 EST memory-label review:** new [IMG_1316](PHOTOS3/IMG_1316.jpeg) shows installed **Micron MTA36ASF4G72PZ-2G6E1 / 32 GB / CPN 15-107071-01**. Manufacturer family specification: DDR4-2666, registered ECC, dual rank, 288 pins, nominal VDD 1.2 V; not a board measurement or flash-programming voltage. Cisco documents a single-DIMM 32 GB option. Exact CPN qualification, SPD contents and health remain unverified; both end latches/contact condition are not established by the image. Requested: note initial latch/alignment state, inspect and reseat the same DIMM once with power/cables absent and ESD precautions, restore cover, remain off for captured retest. Stop if contact/socket damage is found. No completed reseat or post-reseat test is claimed. [Source review and original hash](research-evidence/c8300-a-review-20261102/memory-review.json).

**November 2, 17:16 EST update:** owner identifies chassis **FDO25150HVC** and confirms power off, all cables disconnected and cover still on. No console session remains open. Requested top-cover removal with ESD precautions, then inspection of the still-seated DIMM: both latches, even insertion, accessible capacity/part label. Leave battery/heatsinks/PSU covers fitted; keep all external power/cables disconnected. Cover removal and inspection results remain pending; no reseat or internal electrical/flash operation performed. [Current state record](c8300-recovery/console-20261102/results.json).

**Historical request, October 24; photographs received/reviewed November 2:** identify the full chassis PID suffix and preserve initial hardware condition in photographs. Create/place original images in [Photos/C8300](Photos/C8300/README.md): chassis label, one complete overhead motherboard view, and readable closer views of the RAM and the PSU-to-logic-board connection/regulator area. With all power inputs and cables disconnected, remove only the chassis top cover using [Cisco's internal-component procedure](https://www.cisco.com/c/en/us/td/docs/routers/cloud_edge/c8300/hardware_installation/b-catalyst-8300-series-edge-platforms-hig/m-install-internal-modules-FRUs.html). Keep PSU covers closed and leave heatsinks, RAM, and other components installed for the initial pictures. Do not power the router with the cover removed; no internal energized measurement is currently requested.

**November 2 review:** all ten originals are preserved in PHOTOS3; see the [photo inventory and component findings](Photos/C8300/README.md). U1_BS/U2_BS are identified Micron 32 MiB, 2.7–3.6 V, wide SOIC-16 devices. CPU boot-storage role is inferred, not established by a read. U4_UC/U5_UC are a separate 16 MiB Macronix pair near Microsemi U2_UC. No obvious gross burn/disconnected fan plug is visible; photos cannot verify DIMM contacts or rail health. BOOT OK/BOOT FAIL/PWR OK labels are readable, but no powered states or diagnostic truth table are established. Earlier unknown-part status is superseded; present isolation, pin mapping and contents remain pending.

No G2 firmware file is a restore image for this platform. The 8-pin clip being mechanically compatible does not establish electrical compatibility, boot-flash identity, isolation, or availability of valid replacement firmware. If inspection points toward power/reset faults, define specific low-voltage test points and expected values before powered probing; do not guess rail voltages or work inside mains PSUs.

### C8300-03 — Supplied package analysis and staged troubleshooting

**November 2, 18:42 EST stopping point — C8300-03-READ-PREP:** owner confirms the installed 250W PSU is the same physical unit previously booting C8375; no independent replacement comparison is available. This lowers suspicion of a gross PSU failure without proving load-dependent regulation or internal rails. Pi 5, wide SOIC-16 clip and multimeter are available. Owner requests a plan and stops hardware work until tomorrow. C8300 last confirmed off/unplugged; cover and coin-cell removal were requested but not confirmed. All agent console sessions are closed. No C8300 programmer connection, electrical measurement, flash read/write or new boot occurred. [Detailed plan, state and source references](c8300-recovery/flash-read-preparation-20261102/results.json).

**Tomorrow's staged test:**

1. Reconfirm **FDO25150HVC**, actual power/cables/cover/battery state and no attached programmer. Do not assume the unconfirmed isolation steps were completed.
2. Prepare unpowered measurements: record meter/lead settings and baseline; identify **U1_BS/U2_BS** and each pin-1 mark; verify absent residual voltage before resistance/continuity. Remove the coin-cell for electrical isolation when performed, recording orientation; retain DIMM/heatsinks.
3. Qualify a **3.3 V-class** source, signal levels, clip mapping/contact, control pins and board loading. These **MT25QL256ABA8ESF** devices specify **2.7–3.6 V**, unlike the G2 1.8 V devices. Pi signal compatibility alone does not qualify power delivery. Do not reuse the G2 reset pulldown/adapter unchanged, the unsafe bare CH341A, or guessed headers.
4. After qualification, identify one physical chip read-only, then repeat bounded samples below/above 16 MiB. If stable and plausible, preserve **two full 33,554,432-byte reads per chip**, with separate names and hashes; verify coverage and no aliasing. Stop on unstable reads or electrical loading problems rather than raising voltage or adding router power.
5. Analyze copies offline for firmware structure, versions and available integrity checks. Chip/version differences alone do not prove corruption; the extracted **8 MiB curie1ru update is not a complete 32 MiB restore**. This plan includes no firmware write. RAM health and CPU/DRAM rails remain unresolved.

**Later November 2 extraction supersedes firmware-family uncertainty:** the newly supplied IOS XE 26.2.1 contains `firmware_curie1ru.pkg`, selected for RADIUM/THALLIUM (C8300_1N1S) by Cisco's script. Its CE9331 payload contains the decompressed **26.1(1r) RELEASE SOFTWARE** banner. The CE9351/curie2ru package is selected for URANIUM/THORIUM (2N2S), so it is not the selected reference for this router. All 20 1RU and eight 2RU embedded checksum checks pass; signature authentication remains separate. Full source hash, commands, selection evidence and exact artifacts: [canonical 1RU reference](ROMMON-extracted/classic-c8300/iosxe-26.02.01/curie1ru/README.md). **8,388,608-byte upgrade payload and 17,780,692-byte capsule are not whole-chip restore candidates for the identified 32 MiB devices.** Prior standalone-package findings below remain historical.

**Completed offline, November 2:** inspected `/Volumes/CISCO FILES/C8000-2N2S-17-18-1r.pkg` without changing the USB. Canonical [package, payloads and provenance](ROMMON-extracted/classic-c8300/C8000-2N2S-17-18-1r/README.md), [machine-readable results](research-evidence/c8300-a-review-20261102/results.json). Package SHA-256: `c8844d91e2d64ddff5b939fd538221ff03b5d8fcd64c4f33634de87bf73b394d`. ISO begins at 1084 bytes. URANIUM/THORIUM paths hold identical **25,165,824-byte upgrade** and **34,328,060-byte capsule** payloads; all eight embedded MD5/SHA-1 checks pass. Local copies equal source bytes. Download-catalog checksum, Cisco signature authentication, internal ROMMON release and exact 1N1S board compatibility remain unresolved. Metadata's legacy ISR4300/x86_64/build-version fields are retained without overinterpreting them.

**Decision:** corruption remains plausible, not diagnosed. The C8375 comparison proves that restoring its own original U2 recovered that chassis after its ROMMON experiment; identical-looking symptoms on this classic C8300 do not establish the same failure. Steady red is the documented booting state, distinct from blinking red hardware-integrity indication. Firmware, CPU/DRAM power/reset/clock, memory and console-path faults remain alternatives. Green PWR/FAN does not prove every processor rail is valid.

**Diagnostic sequence:** the initial offline review performed no hardware steps. Later live console and owner-reported DIMM inspection/reseat results are recorded above; internal electrical measurements and flash acquisition remain pending.

1. **Confirm the target/state.** Bind C8300-A to chassis serial and PSU/module labels; record present cover/cooling, power/cables, battery and exclusive console owner. Photo condition is historical. Never run/import old console bridges to discover ownership.
2. **Finish only missing console controls.** Preserve the already-confirmed USB-disconnected RJ45 attempt. Confirm correct console jack and a cable/adapter proven on a working router. Capture from before a normal assembled power-on at 9600/8N1/no flow control; record LEDs and actual received bytes at 1/5/10 minutes. If needed, a controlled 115200 startup and separate mini-USB data-cable/enumeration test follow. Distinguish zero bytes from gibberish or a banner/halt. No repeated equivalent resets if these controls were already done.
3. **Memory and connections.** With all native/external power and cables disconnected, use ESD precautions and Cisco's DIMM procedure to record the installed part, inspect latches/contacts and reseat once. Restore cover/cooling before retest. If needed, compare supported known-good RAM, one variable at a time. Do not substitute G2 RAM without compatibility evidence. Optional modules can be isolated later with correct blanks/cooling. Do not remove the battery as a speculative firmware reset.
4. **Resolve early hardware startup.** Define verified low-voltage ground/test points and expected ranges before any powered rail test; printed rail names are leads, not measurements. A qualified scope/logic-analyzer examination of power-good/reset, clock and BS flash activity could distinguish startup blocked before flash access from an access/firmware failure. Static DC readings alone cannot establish timing; flash traffic alone cannot prove valid firmware. No probe points, header jumpers or open-cover power test are specified for immediate action in this review. Keep PSU covers closed; install insulated leads only with power absent when a specific measurement is later selected.
5. **Preserve BS flash independently.** After isolation/voltage/signal/loading/orientation checks, acquire at least two matching plausible full **33,554,432-byte** reads of **each** U1_BS and U2_BS. Record identity and available read-only status/configuration/OTP separately; main-array dumps need not include them. Check full address coverage, including above 16 MiB, and guard against repeated/aliased halves or all-FF/00 false reads. Keep chip-specific names/hashes; identical reads alone do not prove accuracy. If in-circuit supply collapses or contention appears, stop and establish isolation; no higher voltage or simultaneous router/programmer power.
6. **Choose firmware only from evidence.** The later extraction resolves the vendor-selected 1N1S reference as CE9331/curie1ru 26.1(1r). Compare preserved chip structures/versions and define every changed/preserved region before selecting a restore. Neither that 8 MiB upgrade nor the earlier supplied 24 MiB CE9351 payload establishes a 32 MiB chip layout. Never write the .pkg/capsule directly or pad a payload into a guessed layout. Preserve unique identity/security data; no TPM/fuse/lifecycle changes. If a valid native console/updater becomes available, prefer the documented platform-specific update path. Any eventual write requires full readback followed by a separate normal assembled boot/stability test.

No read, write, DIMM reseat, electrical measurement or boot test was performed in the initial offline review. Subsequent assembled console startups are recorded under C8300-01. No physical bank, debug pinout or selector has been assigned. The subsequent same-DIMM reseat and captured comparison are complete with unchanged silence. The proposed PSU comparison was unavailable because the owner confirms the same shared physical unit. Current work is stopped for tonight; the staged read-only flash plan above requires fresh isolation and electrical qualification before any access.

After boot recovery, define and log an observation duration, reset/reboot behavior, temperature/rail/fan/PSU status, retained configuration, and meaningful physical-port tests. The C8375 previously passed limited two-port tests before the ROMMON experiment; those historical results do not validate its present state. Preserve its user's INSTALL-mode requirement and saved configuration.
